  └ steps\S03.txt (+59 -0)
     1 +Step ID: S03
     2 +
     3 +Why this is one minimal logical deduction unit:
     4 +This passage has one nontrivial output: the compactly supported Lefschetz number of the order-2 deck transformat
        ion is nonzero.
     5 +
     6 +Original step:
     7 +The fundamental group Gamma acts on X by deck transformations. Because M is a manifold, this properly discontinu
        ous action is strictly free. Therefore, the torsion element gamma acts on X as a free, finite-order homeomorphis
        m. As a homeomorphism of X, gamma induces a linear pullback automorphism gamma^* on the compactly supported coho
        mology H_c^*(X;Q). Because H_c^n(X;Q) is a 1-dimensional Q-vector space and gamma^2=id, the induced map gamma^*
        must act as multiplication by +/-1. We evaluate the compactly supported Lefschetz number of gamma, defined algeb
        raically as the alternating sum of the traces of the induced maps on cohomology. This collapses to a single non-
        vanishing term at k=n, yielding:
     8 +
     9 +L_c(gamma,X)=sum_{k=0}^n (-1)^k Tr(gamma^*|H_c^k(X;Q))=(-1)^n(+/-1)!=0.
    10 +
    11 +Target assertion a_i:
    12 +L_c(gamma,X) = (-1)^n(+/-1) and in particular L_c(gamma,X) != 0.
    13 +

        f^{-1}(K) is contained entirely in the finite union of compact sets union_{g in Phi} g.D_X. Therefore, f^{-1}(K)

     7 +*First Proof of Theorem 1.* Suppose for the sake of contradiction that there exists a compact manifold without b
        oundary M of dimension n such that its fundamental group Gamma=pi_1(M) contains an element gamma of order 2, and
         its universal cover X=\tilde{M} is acyclic over the rationals Q. Since Gamma contains a 2-torsion element, M is
         not a point, so n>=1. Because M is a topological manifold, its universal cover X is inherently a simply connect
        ed, and hence orientable, n-dimensional topological manifold.

     1 +Step ID: S01b
     2 +
     3 +Why this is one minimal logical deduction unit:
     4 +This unit isolates the orientability conclusion, which is the only nontrivial external burden left in the openin
        g setup.
     5 +
     6 +Original step:
     7 +Because M is a topological manifold, its universal cover X is inherently a simply connected, and hence orientabl
        e, n-dimensional topological manifold.
     8 +
     9 +Target assertion a_i:
    10 +X is orientable.
    11 +
    12 +Original route:
    13 +Use simple connectivity of X to deduce orientability.
    14 +
    15 +Explanation:
    16 +Once X is known to be a simply connected n-manifold, the source text appeals to the standard orientability fact
        for simply connected manifolds. That is the only external ingredient in this sentence.
    17 +
    18 +Statement Extraction `(Gamma_i, Sigma_i, T_i)`:
    19 +
    20 +Assertion:
    21 +X is orientable.
    22 +
    23 +Local Context `Gamma_i`:
    24 +- gamma_1: X is a connected simply connected n-dimensional topological manifold.
    25 +
    26 +Global Knowledge `Sigma_i`:
    27 +- sigma_1:
    28 +  exact as-used statement: Every connected simply connected topological n-manifold is orientable.
    29 +  proof or precise source: Proof below.
    30 +  why it applies here: gamma_1 identifies X exactly as a connected simply connected manifold, so this theorem yi
        elds orientability of X.
    31 +  status: confirmed
    32 +
    33 +Sigma Support:
    34 +- sigma_1:
    35 +  proof: For a topological manifold X, the local orientation groups H_n(X,X-{x};Z) form a locally constant Z-bun
        dle over X. Fix x_0 in X and choose one of the two generators of H_n(X,X-{x_0};Z). Transport that choice along a
         path from x_0 to any x; local constancy gives a generator in H_n(X,X-{x};Z). If two paths from x_0 to x are giv
        en, simple connectivity makes them homotopic rel endpoints, so the transported generator is the same for both pa
        ths. Thus every point receives a path-independent local orientation, and these local orientations are compatible
         on overlaps. Therefore X is orientable.

    35 +  proof: Let x in S and suppose g in Gamma_0 fixes x. Proper discontinuity implies the stabilizer (Gamma_0)_x is

        nt set S^{gamma_S} is non-empty. Selecting a fixed point p in S^{gamma_S}...
     8 +

        anifold: the midpoint of a geodesic from x to gamma_S x is fixed.

        to x. By uniqueness of geodesics with the same endpoints, gamma_S(c(t))=c(1-t) for all t. Therefore the midpoint

     1 +Step ID: S16c
     2 +
     3 +Why this is one minimal logical deduction unit:
     4 +This unit keeps only the cohomological computation for the linear involution and the resulting compactly support
        ed Lefschetz number.
     5 +
     6 +Original step:
     7 +Its +1 eigenspace has some dimension k (0<=k<=d) corresponding to the tangent space of S^{gamma_S}, and its -1 e
        igenspace has dimension d-k. The induced action of gamma_S on the top compactly supported cohomology H_c^d(S;Q)
        congruent to Q is equivalent to multiplication by the sign of the determinant of this linear involution, which i
        s precisely (-1)^{d-k}. Thus, its compactly supported Lefschetz number evaluates algebraically to:
     8 +
     9 +L_c(gamma_S,S)=(-1)^d Tr(gamma_S^*|H_c^d(S;Q))=(-1)^d (-1)^{d-k}=(-1)^k != 0.
    10 +
    11 +Target assertion a_i:
    12 +L_c(gamma_S,S)=(-1)^k and in particular L_c(gamma_S,S)!=0.
    13 +
    14 +Original route:
    15 +Compute the determinant sign of the linear involution and translate it into the action on top compactly supporte
        d cohomology.
    16 +
    17 +Explanation:
    18 +After linearization, gamma_S acts as a linear involution A on T_pS congruent to R^d. The sign of det(A) governs
        the action on top compactly supported cohomology. Since A has +1-eigenspace of dimension k and -1-eigenspace of
        dimension d-k, det(A)=(-1)^{d-k}. The compactly supported Lefschetz number then follows from the degree-d trace.
    19 +
    20 +Statement Extraction `(Gamma_i, Sigma_i, T_i)`:
    21 +
    22 +Assertion:
    23 +L_c(gamma_S,S)=(-1)^k != 0.
    24 +
    25 +Local Context `Gamma_i`:
    26 +- gamma_1: Under the identification S congruent to T_pS congruent to R^d, gamma_S acts as a linear involution A.
    27 +- gamma_2: The +1 eigenspace of A has dimension k and the -1 eigenspace has dimension d-k.
    28 +
    29 +Global Knowledge `Sigma_i`:
    30 +- sigma_1:
    31 +  exact as-used statement: For a linear automorphism A of R^d, the induced action on H_c^d(R^d;Q) is multiplicat
        ion by sign(det A); equivalently, if A is an involution with +1 eigenspace of dimension k, then the induced acti
        on is multiplication by (-1)^{d-k}.
    32 +  proof or precise source: Proof below.
    33 +  why it applies here: gamma_1 and gamma_2 put the action of gamma_S exactly into this linear form.
    34 +  status: confirmed

    37 +- sigma_1:
    38 +  proof: One-point compactification gives H_c^m(R^d;Q)\cong \widetilde H^m(S^d;Q), so H_c^m(R^d;Q)=0 for m!=d an
        d H_c^d(R^d;Q)\cong Q. A linear automorphism A extends to a continuous map \bar A:S^d->S^d fixing the point at i
        nfinity. Under the compactification isomorphism, the action of A on H_c^d(R^d;Q) is the action of \bar A on \wid
        etilde H^d(S^d;Q). By polar decomposition, A is homotopic through invertible linear maps to an orthogonal matrix
         U, and the degree of the induced sphere map equals deg(U)=sign(det U)=sign(det A). Therefore A^* on H_c^d(R^d;Q
        ) is multiplication by sign(det A). If A is an involution with +1-eigenspace dimension k, then its eigenvalues a
        re +1 with multiplicity k and -1 with multiplicity d-k, so sign(det A)=(-1)^{d-k}.
    39 +
    40 +Background Theory `T_i`:
    41 +- t_1: The compactly supported Lefschetz number is the alternating sum of traces on H_c^m.
    42 +- t_2: If the only nonzero compactly supported cohomology group is in degree d, the Lefschetz number reduces to
        (-1)^d times the trace in degree d.
    43 +- t_3: Because (-1)^k is either +1 or -1, it is nonzero.
    44 +

        ish. Therefore t_1 and t_2 give
    47 +L_c(gamma_S,S)=(-1)^d(-1)^{d-k}=(-1)^k.
    48 +By t_3 this is nonzero.

     1 +Step ID: S09
     2 +
     3 +Why this is one minimal logical deduction unit:
     4 +This unit isolates the virtual torsion-freeness claim itself. The later orientation-preserving finite-index refi
        nement is separated into S09b.
     5 +
     6 +Original step:
     7 +Since Gamma is a finitely generated lattice in a Lie group G with finitely many connected components, its image
        under the adjoint representation Ad:G->GL(g) is a finitely generated linear group. By Selberg's Lemma, Ad(Gamma)
         contains a torsion-free normal subgroup of finite index. Because the kernel of the adjoint representation is th
        e finite center Z(G), pulling this subgroup back to Gamma and intersecting it with a subgroup avoiding the finit
        e central torsion ensures Gamma itself is virtually torsion-free. Thus, Gamma contains a torsion-free normal sub
        group Gamma_0 of finite index.
     8 +
     9 +Target assertion a_i:
    10 +Gamma contains a torsion-free normal subgroup Gamma_0 of finite index.
    11 +
    12 +Original route:
    13 +Use the standard linear-group/Selberg route to deduce virtual torsion-freeness.
    14 +
    15 +Explanation:
    16 +With the standing convention for this verifier run, Gamma is already linear over a characteristic-0 field. That
        lets this step close by the same Selberg-lemma route without using the faulty intermediate sentence about ker(Ad
        ). So the adjoint-representation detour is unnecessary here.
    17 +
    18 +Statement Extraction `(Gamma_i, Sigma_i, T_i)`:

    21 +Gamma contains a torsion-free normal finite-index subgroup Gamma_0.
    22 +
    23 +Local Context `Gamma_i`:
    24 +- gamma_1: Gamma is finitely generated.
    25 +- gamma_2: By the standing convention in this verifier context, Gamma admits a faithful representation rho:Gamma
        ->GL_m(F) over a field F of characteristic 0.
    26 +
    27 +Global Knowledge `Sigma_i`:
    28 +- sigma_1:
    29 +  exact as-used statement: If A is a finitely generated integral domain of characteristic 0, then GL_n(A) contai
        ns a normal torsion-free subgroup of finite index.
    30 +  proof or precise source: Precise source: Roger C. Alperin, "An elementary account of Selberg's lemma," Enseign
        . Math. (2) 33 (1987), 269-273. The theorem stated at the start of the proof section says: for a finitely genera
        ted integral domain A, GL_n(A) is residually finite, and if A has characteristic 0 then GL_n(A) contains a norma
        l subgroup of finite index which is torsion free.
    31 +  why it applies here: From a faithful characteristic-0 linear representation of the finitely generated group Ga
        mma, the matrix entries of a finite generating set lie in a finitely generated integral domain A, so rho(Gamma)
        is contained in GL_m(A).
    32 +  status: confirmed
    33 +
    34 +Sigma Support:
    35 +- sigma_1: Let S be a finite generating set of Gamma from gamma_1. The finitely many matrix entries appearing in
         rho(S) generate a finitely generated subring A of F. Because A is a subring of the field F, it is an integral d
        omain, and because F has characteristic 0, so does A. Every element of rho(Gamma) is a word in rho(S), hence has
         all matrix entries in A. Therefore rho(Gamma) is a subgroup of GL_m(A). Alperin's theorem now applies to GL_m(A
        ) and gives a normal finite-index torsion-free subgroup N of GL_m(A).
    36 +
    37 +Background Theory `T_i`:
    38 +- t_1: The intersection of a subgroup with a normal finite-index subgroup is normal and finite-index in that sub
        group.
    39 +- t_2: The inverse image of a torsion-free subgroup under an injective homomorphism is torsion-free.
    40 +
    41 +Deduction:
    42 +Choose a finite generating set S of Gamma as in gamma_1. By the Sigma Support paragraph, rho(Gamma) is a subgrou
        p of GL_m(A) for some finitely generated integral domain A of characteristic 0. Apply sigma_1 to get a normal fi
        nite-index torsion-free subgroup N triangleleft GL_m(A). Set
    43 +H = rho(Gamma) intersect N.
    44 +By t_1, H is normal and finite-index in rho(Gamma), and because H is contained in the torsion-free group N, H is
         torsion-free. Now define
    45 +Gamma_0 = rho^{-1}(H).
    46 +Since rho is injective by gamma_2, t_2 shows that Gamma_0 is torsion-free. It is normal in Gamma because H is no
        rmal in rho(Gamma), and it has finite index because H has finite index in rho(Gamma). Therefore Gamma contains a
         torsion-free normal subgroup Gamma_0 of finite index.
    47 +
